Read each selected requirement's (REQ) acceptance criteria and linked\\n   specifications (SPEC).\\n2. Compare those criteria with the existing verification contracts (VER).\\n3. Select unchanged contracts that cover the proposed behavior.\\n4. For each coverage gap, create a verification contract (VER) with the\\n   commands below, or use [Propose any needed amendments to existing definitions](AMEND_DEFINITIONS.md#propose-any-needed-amendments-to-existing-definitions) to propose an amendment to an existing one.\\n5. Complete `Requirement-to-evidence matrix`. For each requirement (REQ),\\n   record its ID, method, case or evidence, and pass condition. Use `test`,\\n   `analysis`, `inspection`, or `demonstration` as the method.\\n6. Specify the inputs, platform, evaluator, and command or manual actions for\\n   each check. Derive expected results from the requirement (REQ) and\\n   specification (SPEC), not from candidate output.\\n7. Set `[relations].verifies` to the covered requirement (REQ) IDs.\\n8. Complete `Independence`, `Evidence retention`, and applicable assessment\\n   sections in each new or edited draft.\\n9. Apply `## verification` \\u2192 `### Checklist` in\\n   `docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md`.\\n10. Retain questions about missing criteria or methods for [Record unresolved decisions (DEC)](RISKS_AND_DECISIONS.md#record-unresolved-decisions-dec).\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\nFor each new verification contract (VER), run the creation sequence from\\n[Draft any missing definitions](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#draft-any-missing-definitions) with these arguments. Inspect the preview before creation; replace\\n`VER-ID` with its `allocated_id`:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type verification --dry-run --json\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type verification --id VER-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nNo harness command writes the verification method or pass criteria. The human\\nor agent completes that content. No verification record (VREC) is created here.\\n\\n**Completion:** Every selected requirement (REQ) has a defined verification\\nmethod and pass condition, or an explicit unresolved question. Each proposed\\ncheck identifies how to perform it and where its evidence will be retained.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Prepare work orders (WO) linking the definitions, scope, planned work, and verification requirements](DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md#prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements) links these contracts to work orders (WO). The execution\\nand verification processes later perform the checks and assess the evidence.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"16a9ea7323450e244b26edd2d1ad757048124b627bcfffa157c8b173f57f363c\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-authority38-20261012\\\\claude-341\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"71de2cc92a470e2da25d525e3ad002b77faedb6ef0e69e2fa476a2fa4cb78ff5\", \"bytes\": 11691, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"design-simplicity\", \"title\": \"Design simplicity\", \"anchor\": \"design-simplicity\", \"start_line\": 21, \"end_line\": 58, \"content\": \"## Design simplicity\\n\\nFor unfinished work, `harnessctl validate-draft REPO --artifact ID --json`\\nreports whether one supported draft has an admissible shape. Only exact values\\nat the same type and field in this evaluator's canonical template, and missing\\nor empty required authoring links, can be reported as incomplete. Invalid\\nidentity, dates, metadata shapes, actual endpoints, lifecycle history and\\ndisposition are errors. A missing non-template ID is an error. Background\\nfindings remain visible; this result makes no whole-repository validity claim.\\nComplete the authoring checklist and pass ordinary approval gates before\\nrequesting approval. Draft admission grants no authority to write or approve.\\n\\nChoose the least complex design that satisfies the agreed requirements and\\nconstraints. Justify material added complexity by the need it serves and why\\na simpler approach is insufficient. Consider the effort to understand, build,\\ntest, operate and change the whole solution. Defer speculative needs. Review\\nboth the necessity of the specified behavior and the simplicity of its\\nimplementation. Preserve correctness and the project's required quality and\\nsafety properties.\\n\\nStart from the smallest complete solution. Each project supplies its agreed\\nusers, scale, operating conditions and quality constraints. Agreed future needs\\ncan matter; imagined flexibility alone is insufficient. Credible risk analysis\\ncan justify prevention before an incident. Fewer lines, shorter specifications\\nor fewer tests do not establish simplicity; moving work to users, operations\\nor dependencies also has a cost.\\n\\nApply the relevant questions below before definition approval and during design\\nand implementation review. Return unjustified obligations or design complexity\\nfor revision through the existing decision process. Explain material choices\\nin the existing specification, design or review: the need, why a simpler option\\nis insufficient and the added cost. Scale the explanation to the decision; a\\nsmall choice may need one sentence, a significant design may use its existing\\narchitecture decision. Trivial changes need no separate comparison document.\\n\\nThese are human review standards. They introduce no separate KISS artifact,\\nmandatory score, receipt, automated simplicity gate or additional approval.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"29e9b6296f39215c4b2ad36659c0674f0b20650682db9aa1187fc97871cfdb06\"}, {\"heading\": \"intent\", \"title\": \"intent\", \"anchor\": \"intent\", \"start_line\": 76, \"end_line\": 84, \"content\": \"## intent\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- Explain the present problem, who it affects, and the outcome wanted.\\n- What is the smallest useful outcome, and which needs and constraints are actually agreed?\\n- Say how the owner will know the change helped; an honest baseline may be `not measured`.\\n- State the important scope limits. Create a new intent when the desired outcome changes.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"291e8274f0c400a3d2d1ecdd56f2c10d68ab6cb40f667076a034e44d87c05e42\"}, {\"heading\": \"verification\", \"title\": \"verification\", \"anchor\": \"verification\", \"start_line\": 126, \"end_line\": 135, \"content\": \"## verification\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- Independence: expected values derive from the requirements and specifications, never from candidate output.\\n- A requirement-to-evidence matrix with a pass condition per requirement.\\n- Cover normal behavior and meaningful failure outcomes or risks; do not invent failure scenarios to fill a template.\\n- What accepted behavior or quality does each test or CI check establish? Cover distinct relevant outcomes, reuse applicable evidence, and avoid mirroring every implementation branch or combination.\\n- Pass criteria name the platforms and the evaluator.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"e6d36e5fe7a23845861e67079317f856b4f892d7f17c4f3d4dbdb4476edb7720\"}, {\"heading\": \"work_order\", \"title\": \"work_order\", \"anchor\": \"work_order\", \"start_line\": 136, \"end_line\": 146, \"content\": \"## work_order\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- `[execution_scope].paths` is exact: files or component-prefix directories, nothing wider.\\n- `[assurance]` is classified and its rationale names what later decisions depend on.\\n- In scope and out of scope are both stated; the decision envelope says what the implementer may and may not decide.\\n- Stop conditions and the completion report format are stated.\\n- Is every planned change needed for the agreed outcome? Include removal or adjustment of obsolete behavior, tests and instructions within the approved scope.\\n- Inspect supporting paths before approval and assess the supplied plan through [Draft work orders](harness/DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md#prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements). Keep reasons in Expected change surface; do not add a separate planning artifact.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"e8d3be9bfae6d0679a08544ac587fa73e1a26e026d23373c29e1927e0803e7b8\"}, {\"heading\": \"requirement\", \"title\": \"requirement\", \"anchor\": \"requirement\", \"start_line\": 59, \"end_line\": 75, \"content\": \"## requirement\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- State the observable behavior and why it is needed. Ordinary language is accepted; `SHALL` is optional.\\n- What agreed outcome does this obligation serve, for whom and under which conditions? Separate requirements from assumptions and speculative needs.\\n- Include a concrete acceptance condition in `Examples`, `Acceptance`, `measure`, `verification_notes`, or a linked verification contract.\\n- Name the source capability and a verification method. Supported method arrays contain `test`, `analysis`, `inspection`, or `demonstration`; retained text methods remain readable.\\n- Replace unfinished placeholders and resolve blocking decisions before approval.\\n\\n### Guidance\\n\\nWrite enough for someone to implement and check the behavior. Use the template's\\nsections when helpful. Word counts, sentence shapes and numbers of code references\\nare not checked. Writing hints never block approval. Optional metadata must still\\nhave a valid value when supplied.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"333872b200842af881c6c22cfcb0631897071421f423885b2702ac6b72c75770\"}, {\"heading\": \"capability\", \"title\": \"capability\", \"anchor\": \"capability\", \"start_line\": 85, \"end_line\": 93, \"content\": \"## capability\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- Say what an actor should be able to do and any relevant conditions.\\n- Does each supported case serve an agreed need under those conditions?\\n- Link the intent it serves. The graph lists the derived requirements.\\n- Put implementation details where they help the implementer; no required `can ... under` wording.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"ee6452eeeb618cbe10c941638e7f6be38d2709f53e169920c2d3d012c94ce978\"}, {\"heading\": \"specification\", \"title\": \"specification\", \"anchor\": \"specification\", \"start_line\": 94, \"end_line\": 107, \"content\": \"## specification\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- Explain what an implementation must do, including meaningful failure behavior.\\n- Is each promised behavior necessary? Could a simpler contract meet the agreed need? Prescribe a mechanism only when it is a necessary constraint.\\n- Link the requirements and show an example someone can check.\\n- Give a rule a stable identifier when evidence or a decision needs to refer to it.\\n- Check that supplied rule references exist and identifiers are unambiguous.\\n\\nMissing summaries and ambiguous rule references may produce draft hints. Hints\\nhelp a reviewer; they do not withhold approval. Existing approved text need not\\nbe rewritten to adopt these checklists.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"7aeefa5f4ed363ecdef5185d2b776c6fdd9146ece8e2bfd7ae1ee852f0acf6e1\"}]}]}",
  "is_error": false
}

It does not\\nmean every change must create a new file. Reuse an existing active artifact\\nwhen its accepted meaning and scope still cover the work. Create a conditional\\nartifact only when its condition applies.\\n\\nFor an existing selected work order (WO), get its current state and next\\naction with:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nBefore implementation, check whether that work order (WO) and its linked\\ndefinitions are eligible to start:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase start --json\\n```\\n\\nThe first command reports lifecycle context without evaluating checkpoint\\ngates. The second evaluates start readiness, including the states and coverage\\nof the selected definitions. Neither command approves or starts work.\\nHere, `REPO` is the absolute repository path and `WO-ID` is the actual ID of\\nthe selected work order (WO). Use the repository's selected released evaluator.\\n\\n| Type                  | ID prefix | Purpose                                                                                                                                                                      | When needed                                                                                                                                              |\\n|-----------------------|-----------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\\n| `intent`              | `INT-`    | States the problem, intended outcome, and limits of responsibility.                                                                                                          | The work needs an accepted purpose. Reuse an intent (INT) when that purpose still applies.                                                               |\\n| `capability`          | `CAP-`    | Describes an ability that a human or agent should have.                                                                                                                      | Requirements (REQ) need a link to the capability (CAP) they support.                                                                                     |\\n| `requirement`         | `REQ-`    | States required behavior or a constraint, with a condition for acceptance.                                                                                                   | The change creates or changes an obligation. Reuse a requirement (REQ) only if its meaning stays unchanged.                                              |\\n| `specification`       | `SPEC-`   | Defines the detailed behavior, interfaces, constraints, and rejection conditions.                                                                                            | Each requirement (REQ) selected for implementation needs specification (SPEC) coverage.                                                                  |\\n| `architecture`        | `ARCH-`   | Defines system structure and boundaries needed to meet significant requirements (REQ).                                                                                       | An active architecture (ARCH) directly addresses a requirement (REQ) selected by the work order (WO).                                                    |\\n| `adr`                 | `ADR-`    | Records an architecture decision (ADR), the alternatives, and their consequences.                                                                                            | An applicable architecture (ARCH) declares that a significant decision is required.                                                                      |\\n| `verification`        | `VER-`    | Defines how requirements (REQ) will be checked and what counts as a pass.                                                                                                    | Each selected requirement (REQ) needs verification coverage. This is the verification contract (VER), not a test result.                                 |\\n| `work_order`          | `WO-`     | Defines the bounded work to execute and its governing artifacts. Approval grants work authority.                                                                             | Before execution. One work order (WO) may cover several requirements (REQ). Completed scope does not authorize later work.                               |\\n| `verification_record` | `VREC-`   | Binds work orders (WO), verification contracts (VER), evidence, and one exact candidate commit for assessment.                                                               | When verification tied to a commit is required. One record may cover several work orders (WO) at the same commit.                                        |\\n| `release_contract`    | `REL-`    | Defines permitted release scope, gates, rollback conditions, and authority limits.                                                                                           | When a release record (RLS) is proposed.                                                                                                                 |\\n| `release_record`      | `RLS-`    | Records preparation and the later release decision for verified work at one exact commit.                                                                                    | When a formal release is proposed. Preparing the record does not release the work.                                                                       |\\n| `decision`            | `DEC-`    | Records a question, options, recommendation, decision maker, affected artifacts, and the eventual answer. It can also record a deviation from one specification (SPEC) rule. | A question blocks a transition, affects more than one artifact, or must remain recorded after approval; or work cannot meet a specification (SPEC) rule. |\\n| `risk`                | `RISK-`   | Records a threat, an owner, and a next action. Scoring and categories are optional.                                                                                          | A concern needs follow-up. A separate decision (DEC) is used when the concern must block work.                                                           |\\n| `operating_contract`  | `OPS-`    | Defines continuing service, support, monitoring, or operational assurance obligations.                                                                                       | Ongoing operational commitments apply. Omitting the record does not prove those commitments have been met.                                               |\\n\\nCode, tests, acceptance scenarios, evidence files, commits, dashboards, tickets,\\nand conversations are not additional formal artifact types. They may support\\nor be referenced by formal artifacts but do not grant authority by themselves.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"47268c9ec1e3551fb547ad3d1ef7b8da0b773b52b3a4364f42c36cc64f98bf83\"}, {\"heading\": \"artifact-locations\", \"title\": \"Artifact locations\", \"anchor\": \"artifact-locations\", \"start_line\": 63, \"end_line\": 96, \"content\": \"## Artifact locations\\n\\nNew formal artifacts use these directories below\\n`docs/engineering/DOMAIN/`. `DOMAIN` is the selected domain name. These paths\\ndescribe storage, not authority; the artifact ID, metadata, and typed links\\nremain authoritative. Use the supported authoring command to obtain the actual\\npath. Do not move existing records merely to match this table.\\n\\n| Artifact | TYPE | Directory |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| Intent (INT) | `intent` | `intent/` |\\n| Capability (CAP) | `capability` | `capabilities/` |\\n| Requirement (REQ) | `requirement` | `requirements/` |\\n| Specification (SPEC) | `specification` | `specifications/` |\\n| Architecture (ARCH) | `architecture` | `architecture/` |\\n| Architecture decision (ADR) | `adr` | `architecture/adr/` |\\n| Verification contract (VER) | `verification` | `verification/` |\\n| Work order (WO) | `work_order` | `work-orders/` |\\n| Verification record (VREC) | `verification_record` | `verification-records/` |\\n| Release contract (REL) | `release_contract` | `release/` |\\n| Release record (RLS) | `release_record` | `releases/` |\\n| Operating contract (OPS) | `operating_contract` | `operations/` |\\n| Decision (DEC) | `decision` | `decisions/` |\\n| Risk (RISK) | `risk` | `risks/` |\\n\\nThe same domain has `evidence/` for retained work-order evidence and\\n`acceptance/` for acceptance scenarios. These are supporting files, not new\\nformal artifact types. Legacy flat artifact locations remain readable and\\nMUST NOT be moved by an upgrade without separate authority.\\n\\nTemplates under `docs/engineering/templates/` are authoring assets. They are\\nexcluded from active-artifact validation and grant no authority. Use\\n`capture-verification` and `prepare-release` for commit-bound VREC and RLS\\ninstances; an edited template is not a substitute for their derived evidence.\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"8df276d2f837c4dcc663dbdb42c67cbb6f8fe3bf4abcb53acf86bc7538269a72\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/DEFINITION_LINKS.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-authority38-20261012\\\\claude-341\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\DEFINITION_LINKS.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"75b0c06f90fbb219fc31684a1a2d97987215dbada20e335656e45a57532c3010\", \"bytes\": 3505, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"links-between-definitions\", \"title\": \"Links between definitions\", \"anchor\": \"links-between-definitions\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 35, \"content\": \"## Links between definitions\\n\\nAn arrow points from the artifact that records the link to the artifact it\\nreferences. These links describe meaning and coverage, not execution order.\\n\\nRepository validation, draft validation and work-order preflight share the\\ndeclared source/relation/target types. A supplied undeclared relation or a target\\nof the wrong type is invalid even when no work order selects the artifact.\\nPreflight still evaluates readiness only for its selected governing chain.\\nThe read-only draft query permits specific unfinished authoring slots; ordinary\\nvalidation and approval retain their completeness requirements.\\n\\n```mermaid\\nflowchart LR\\n VER[\\\"Verification contract (VER)\\\"] -->|verifies| REQ\\n    ARCH[\\\"Architecture (ARCH)\\\"] -->|addresses| REQ\\n    ARCH -->|conforms_to| SPEC\\n    ADR[\\\"Architecture decision (ADR)\\\"] -->|decides| ARCH\\n```\\n\\n| Source \\u2192 target | Relation | Meaning and coverage |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| Capability (CAP) \\u2192 Intent (INT) | `derives_from` | Each active capability (CAP) names one or more active intents (INT). |\\n| Requirement (REQ) \\u2192 Capability (CAP) | `derives_from` | Each active requirement (REQ) names one or more active capabilities (CAP). |\\n| Specification (SPEC) \\u2192 Requirement (REQ) | `specifies` | Each requirement (REQ) selected by a work order (WO) needs one or more selected active specifications (SPEC). |\\n| Architecture (ARCH) \\u2192 Requirement (REQ) | `addresses` | The architecture (ARCH) names every requirement (REQ) that materially drives its structure. |\\n| Architecture (ARCH) \\u2192 Specification (SPEC) | `conforms_to` | For each addressed requirement (REQ), the selected architecture (ARCH) names at least one selected specification (SPEC) covering it. |\\n| Architecture decision (ADR) \\u2192 Architecture (ARCH) | `decides` | Each selected architecture (ARCH) that requires a design decision has at least one selected active architecture decision (ADR). |\\n| Verification contract (VER) \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-authority38-20261012\\\\claude-341\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\DEFINE_CHANGE.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"7f41ea6b62df110755524d88293019406d6cf6a238abc67e795db98234024f52\", \"bytes\": 10552, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"read-this-when\", \"title\": \"Read this when\", \"anchor\": \"read-this-when\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 6, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Read this when\\n\\nA new change needs an outcome, limits or selection of existing artifacts.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"c2cb6d6aa5d04e2579b2641ce2d738fd1359afb9a7b30a63652e4dfa3b8db8d9\"}, {\"heading\": \"before-this-action\", \"title\": \"Before this action\", \"anchor\": \"before-this-action\", \"start_line\": 7, \"end_line\": 10, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Before this action\\n\\nBefore selecting artifacts, read [ARTIFACTS.md#artifact-types](ARTIFACTS.md#artifact-types) and [DEFINITION_LINKS.md#links-between-definitions](DEFINITION_LINKS.md#links-between-definitions).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"3ce477685d52f9c993e2006dcb2e0e67c0beb152b3570f7b808f5af8367ec631\"}, {\"heading\": \"identify-the-existing-artifacts-that-apply-to-the-change\", \"title\": \"Identify the existing artifacts that apply to the change\", \"anchor\": \"identify-the-existing-artifacts-that-apply-to-the-change\", \"start_line\": 110, \"end_line\": 177, \"content\": \"### Identify the existing artifacts that apply to the change\\n\\n**Inputs:** The confirmed outcome, scope limits, any artifact IDs supplied\\nin the request, and the existing formal records. Use\\n`docs/engineering/README.md` when locating a domain.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** None created or modified.\\n- **Transient working material:** A list of selected artifacts (the artifact\\n  inventory), a list of missing definitions, and the evaluator results.\\n  These are needed only for the current change-definition context. The agent\\n  SHALL retain them in the conversation or, at its discretion, in a temporary\\n  location outside the repository. They SHALL NOT be persisted in the\\n  repository. The agent chooses whether a temporary file is needed and, if\\n  so, its filename and format.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Run the repository inspection command below.\\n2. If the request selects a work order (WO), run the selected-work command\\n   below. Read that work order (WO) and every file in the returned\\n   `context.reading_manifest`.\\n3. Otherwise, locate the artifacts named in the request. If none are named,\\n   use `docs/engineering/README.md` to locate the domain covering the function\\n   or component in the scope. Read its intents (INT), capabilities (CAP),\\n   and requirements (REQ). Record a missing domain if none covers the change.\\n4. Select the requirements (REQ) whose behavior the change would modify or\\n   reuse. Trace each one back to its capabilities (CAP) and intents (INT).\\n5. Read the specifications (SPEC), applicable architectures (ARCH), architecture\\n   decisions (ADR), verification contracts (VER), and work orders (WO) linked\\n   to those selected requirements (REQ). Use the declared relations listed\\n   in [Definition links](DEFINITION_LINKS.md#links-between-definitions). Keep unrelated work outside the selected scope.\\n6. Read decisions (DEC) and risks (RISK) that name the selected artifacts.\\n7. For each selected artifact, retain its name and code, such as Requirement\\n   (REQ), its ID, file path, current lifecycle state, and why it applies to the\\n   change. Keep this list as transient working material; do not create a\\n   repository inventory file.\\n8. Assign one treatment to each entry: `reuse`, `amend`, or `reference only`.\\n   Use `reuse` only when its existing meaning covers the change unchanged.\\n9. List missing definitions by type and purpose. Identify their intended domain.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\nRepository inspection, used in action 1:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl inspect REPO --json\\n```\\n\\nSelected-work inspection, used in action 2. Replace `WO-ID` with the actual\\nwork order (WO) ID:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nBoth commands are read-only. `inspect` does not select the change scope.\\nThe checkpoint-free `check` reports lifecycle context without evaluating\\ncheckpoint gates. ## Event 135: user tool_result

{
  "tool_use_id": "toolu_01KoqknuEq26PYDXnUGL55XJ",
  "type": "tool_result",
  "content": "{\"resources\": [{\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-authority38-20261012\\\\claude-341\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"c4ffde0efff3a57df41cf6b282896e6827ea289607f8b73faa8f7c09d0c70213\", \"bytes\": 8654, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"read-this-when\", \"title\": \"Read this when\", \"anchor\": \"read-this-when\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 6, \"content\": \"## Read this when\\n\\nDefinitions, work scope and verification requirements are ready for work planning.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"3977586afdaba8e900cfd38bfd72b02ccf3d011048dae4b247fe81a24c64c9cc\"}, {\"heading\": \"before-this-action\", \"title\": \"Before this action\", \"anchor\": \"before-this-action\", \"start_line\": 7, \"end_line\": 10, \"content\": \"## Before this action\\n\\nRead [WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#links-from-work-orders-wo](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#links-from-work-orders-wo), [WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#complete-work-scope](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#complete-work-scope) and [WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification). Apply [../ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md#design-simplicity](../ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md#design-simplicity) and [../ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md#work_order](../ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md#work_order).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"d2a9a7707114a465ebde20884e2a996807663ab4aa94ff11008f2360617efe50\"}, {\"heading\": \"prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements\", \"title\": \"Prepare work orders (WO) linking the definitions, scope, planned work, and verification requirements\", \"anchor\": \"prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements\", \"start_line\": 13, \"end_line\": 143, \"content\": \"### Prepare work orders (WO) linking the definitions, scope, planned work, and verification requirements\\n\\n**Inputs:** The outcome, scope, artifact inventory, definition drafts,\\namendment proposals, verification contracts (VER), risks (RISK), and\\nunresolved decisions (DEC).\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** New or completed work orders (WO) in `draft`.\\n  Applicable risks (RISK) and open decisions (DEC) updated with the actual\\n  work order (WO) links. Lifecycle states and dispositions are preserved.\\n  Approved work orders (WO) remain unchanged; proposed scope changes require\\n  review.\\n- **Transient working material:** The review handoff and evaluator results.\\n  Retain these under the same conditions as [Describe the intended outcome](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#describe-the-intended-outcome).\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Inspect the implementation and follow its callers, references and consumers.\\n   Check tests and fixtures, instructions and documentation, packaging and CI,\\n   and verification outputs where they apply. Keep the working inventory in\\n   the conversation or temporary storage outside the repository.\\n   Group each complete, independently verifiable outcome into one bounded\\n   work order with its necessary supporting changes.\\n2. Select an existing work order (WO) in `draft` for a unit only if its scope\\n   matches. Otherwise, create a new work order (WO) with the commands below.\\n3. Complete each draft using the field table below. In Expected change\\n   surface, name the inspected paths and give a short reason for each.\\n   Explain non-applicable areas and unknown destinations briefly.\\n4. Check that every selected requirement (REQ) has the specification (SPEC)\\n   and verification contract (VER) coverage defined in [Complete work scope](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#complete-work-scope).\\n5. Apply `## work_order` \\u2192 `### Checklist` in\\n   `docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md`.\\n6. Add the new work order (WO) IDs to applicable `threatens` relations in risks\\n   (RISK) and `concerns` or `blocks` relations in open decisions (DEC). Keep\\n   every blocked ID in `concerns`. Preserve any required pairing between the\\n   risk (RISK) and decision (DEC).\\n7. If the planned change needs a missing release or operating contract,\\n   use [Prepare a release or operating contract](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#prepare-a-release-or-operating-contract) now that the work\\n   order IDs exist. Include its draft in the proposed package.\\n8. Run the validation command below.\\n9. Correct findings within the proposed drafts. Rerun validation after edits.\\n   Report unrelated findings separately. Retain findings that need a decision\\n   or an accepted-definition amendment as unresolved dependencies.\\n10. Run the selected-work check below for each proposed work order (WO).\\n    Supply every planned file from Expected change surface with\\n    `--planned-path`. Inspect `scope.preparation`: resolve uncovered or\\n    invalid paths in the draft before requesting approval. A successful\\n    command exit does not establish coverage or a complete impact analysis.\\n    Explain future generated destinations using\\n    [Generated outputs](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#generated-outputs).\\n11. Present the outcome, scope, artifact IDs and paths, validation results,\\n    unresolved decisions (DEC), and unapplied amendments. Include the\\n    evaluator's reported next action for each selected work order (WO).\\n\\n| Field or section | Content |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| `Objective` | The outcome this work order (WO) will deliver. |\\n| `In scope` / `Out of scope` | The boundaries from [Define the limits of the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#define-the-limits-of-the-change) assigned to this work order (WO). |\\n| `Constraints` | The conditions from [Define the limits of the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#define-the-limits-of-the-change) that apply to this work. |\\n| `Expected change surface` | Inspected planned paths, the reason for each, and unresolved destinations. |\\n| `[execution_scope].paths` | Exact repository-relative files or component directories ending in `/`. Use forward slashes; no wildcards, absolute paths, or `..`. |\\n| `Authorized decision envelope` | What the implementer may decide and what requires another accountable decision. |\\n| `Required verification` / `Evidence to record` | The checks and retained outputs defined in [Define how the result will be verified](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#define-how-the-result-will-be-verified). |\\n| `Stop and escalate conditions` | The conditions that require pausing this work. |\\n| `Completion report format` | The required report of completed behavior, checks, limitations, and next accountable decision. |\\n| `[assurance]` | `commit_bound_verification`, its `rationale`, and `decided_by`. Apply the classification rules in [Assurance classification](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification). Record the human who confirmed the classification; an agent may propose it and record that human's decision. |\\n| `[relations]` | `implements` \\u2192 requirements (REQ); `specifications` \\u2192 specifications (SPEC); `verification` \\u2192 verification contracts (VER); `architecture` \\u2192 applicable architectures (ARCH) and required architecture decisions (ADR). |\\n\\nFor PR-based work, include the proposed [review-publication grant](AUTHORITY.md#review-publication-authority)\\nin the decision envelope. Name its repository, source branch, target branch\\nand selected work. Include both the ready-record publication and the later\\ndecision-only push in the expected scope. Present that grant explicitly with\\nimplementation approval; do not infer it from ordinary work authority.\\n\\nIf the assurance classification has not been confirmed, present it as a\\nproposal in the review handoff. Report the missing decision. Do not fill\\n`decided_by` with the draft author's name or an example identity to satisfy\\nvalidation. The human may confirm the classification while reviewing the\\nwork order; record that decision before previewing approval.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\nFor each new work order (WO), run the creation sequence from [Draft any missing definitions](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#draft-any-missing-definitions).\\nInspect the preview before creation; replace `WO-ID` with its `allocated_id`:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type work_order --dry-run --json\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type work_order --id WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nValidate the formal artifacts after completing the drafts and links:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl validate REPO --json\\n```\\n\\nInspect each proposed work order (WO) using its actual ID:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nTo assess the proposed file list, repeat `--planned-path` for every file:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --planned-path PATH --planned-path ANOTHER-PATH --json\\n```\\n\\nThis option is available only when the selected released evaluator supports\\nit. If it is unavailable, report that version gap; do not run candidate\\nsource to govern the repository.\\n\\nThe optional planning view evaluates no checkpoint. It reports explicit\\nscope matches, existing automatic admission, uncovered paths and invalid\\ndeclarations. It does not inspect dependencies for the agent, prove that\\nthe supplied list is complete, or turn planned paths into observed changes.\\nDo not combine it with checkpoint or actual-change inputs.\\n\\nValidation does not approve the change. The checkpoint-free `check` reports\\nlifecycle context without evaluating checkpoint gates.\\n\\n**Completion:** Draft work orders (WO) are presented for review with their\\ngoverning artifacts and evaluator results. Every unresolved dependency is\\nidentified. Preparation does not approve or start execution.\\n\\n**Later use:** The authorization process reviews this artifact package,\\nresolves approval blockers, and applies permitted lifecycle transitions.\\nOnly the formal artifacts carry lasting definitions and work authority;\\ntransient working material is not persisted in the repository.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"ee93a263dcebc650ee59519ecaafa4c005120db60b83b8acc6bf4191efe3c8dd\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-authority38-20261012\\\\claude-341\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"892ec2fedefc272f6bc2580268395e487f392cf5d92a3ea81fac015761bc87a8\", \"bytes\": 8641, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"complete-work-scope\", \"title\": \"Complete work scope\", \"anchor\": \"complete-work-scope\", \"start_line\": 47, \"end_line\": 65, \"content\": \"## Complete work scope\\n\\nEvery requirement (REQ) selected by a work order (WO)\\nMUST link back to active capabilities (CAP) and intents (INT). It MUST also\\nhave selected active specifications (SPEC) and verification contracts (VER).\\nReuse existing artifacts where their scope applies.\\n\\nA work order should cover one complete bounded outcome, including its\\nforeseeable tests, documentation and retained evidence. Use exact file paths\\nby default. Use a narrow existing component directory when the affected\\nfiles belong to that component. A matching directory does not authorize\\nunrelated behavior.\\n\\nPrepare this scope through\\n[Draft work orders](DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md#prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements).\\nDo not split predictable supporting changes into extra work orders merely\\nbecause they use different file types. A genuine expansion after approval\\nstill needs the applicable human decision; preserve the approved history.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"021ca3cdecffebe415ce9df445c0c48e24a0acde7609d25220c0fb7d470e054e\"}]}]}",

  "content": "{\"resources\": [{\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-authority38-20261012\\\\claude-341\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"892ec2fedefc272f6bc2580268395e487f392cf5d92a3ea81fac015761bc87a8\", \"bytes\": 8641, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"links-from-work-orders-wo\", \"title\": \"Links from work orders (WO)\", \"anchor\": \"links-from-work-orders-wo\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 28, \"content\": \"## Links from work orders (WO)\\n\\nA work order (WO) selects the definitions and verification contracts (VER) that govern\\nits execution. Links to requirements (REQ) alone are not enough.\\n\\n| Source \\u2192 target | Relation | Meaning and coverage |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| Work order (WO) \\u2192 Requirement (REQ) | `implements` | Names every requirement (REQ) in the authorized work scope. |\\n| Work order (WO) \\u2192 Specification (SPEC) | `specifications` | Names the specifications (SPEC) governing those requirements (REQ). |\\n| Work order (WO) \\u2192 Architecture (ARCH) or Architecture decision (ADR) | `architecture` | Names every applicable architecture (ARCH) and every required architecture decision (ADR). |\\n| Work order (WO) \\u2192 Verification contract (VER) | `verification` | Names the verification contracts (VER) for the work. |\\n\\nFor example, a work order (WO) can declare these links in its metadata. The IDs\\nbelow are examples, not records created by this document.\\n\\n```toml\\n[relations]\\nimplements = [\\\"REQ-EXAMPLE-001\\\"]\\nspecifications = [\\\"SPEC-EXAMPLE-001\\\"]\\nverification = [\\\"VER-EXAMPLE-001\\\"]\\n```\\n\\nThe referenced requirement (REQ) must also link back to a capability (CAP) and intent (INT).\\nAdd architecture (ARCH) links when architecture (ARCH) applies; do not invent them just to\\nfill a field.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"e5892787048f79f437b0395f9e1269152ce3d1d1b6fb28994f42849bd12a4f54\"}, {\"heading\": \"assurance-classification\", \"title\": \"Assurance classification\", \"anchor\": \"assurance-classification\", \"start_line\": 86, \"end_line\": 116, \"content\": \"## Assurance classification\\n\\nEach work order (WO) in `approved` or\\n`in_progress` MUST set `assurance.commit_bound_verification` to `required`\\nor `not_required`.\\n\\nUse `required` when later decisions depend on the correctness of changes to\\ncode, required checks, CI configuration, harness policy, artifact definitions,\\nor their links. These are examples of \\u201cchanged trusted state\\u201d: information or\\nbehavior that others will rely on when accepting, releasing, or operating the\\nresult.\\n\\nFor example, a work order (WO) changes the code that validates artifact links.\\nA later release decision relies on that validation being correct. The work\\norder (WO) therefore needs verification tied to its exact candidate commit.\\n\\nUse `not_required` only when the work solely records or transports a\\nverification, release, supersession, publication, or deployment decision\\nthat an authorized human has already made. It does not make a new decision\\nor change the definitions, implementation, or evidence behind that decision.\\n\\nFor example, a work order (WO) commits an already authorized release decision\\nin a release record (RLS), with no change to the released code, scope, or\\nsupporting evidence. The work records the decision; it does not reassess the\\nrelease.\\n\\nA work order (WO) that combines both kinds of work MUST be split or classified\\n`required`. `not_required` removes the obligation to prepare a new\\nverification record (VREC) for that work; it does not remove required checks\\nor the links to verification contracts (VER).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"6ad947ba93cfc84640c82a0b165448ce2c15f04beed36f65a3dc031e4287aab3\"}, {\"heading\": \"generated-outputs\", \"title\": \"Generated outputs\", \"anchor\": \"generated-outputs\", \"start_line\": 66, \"end_line\": 85, \"content\": \"## Generated outputs\\n\\nExisting scope rules admit the selected work order's own file, records that\\ndirectly verify or release that work order, and those records' declared\\nevaluator-evidence paths. This does not admit those records' parent directories.\\nThe selected work order's own packet directory is also already admitted by\\nthe existing scope rules; the planning report identifies that separate rule.\\n\\nList other planned evidence, review and log destinations explicitly in the\\nwork order's scope unless an existing rule covers them. A neighboring file\\nor a record linked only to another work order is not automatically admitted.\\nThe conditional rule for recording risks during execution remains unchanged;\\nit is not a blanket admission of future files during preparation.\\n\\nIf a future record's ID is not allocated, mark its destination as unresolved\\nand explain the relationship rule expected to cover it. Do not create a\\nrecord or invent an ID to complete the plan. Once the evaluator prepares\\nthe record, assess its actual returned record and evidence paths. An\\nunresolved destination is not an already covered file.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"21cb2760e5cf61158c5d19cca2af8dbe727ecf3df32247987cc1636059bde3e9\"}]}]}",
  "is_error": false
}

Git remains authoritative\"}, \"documents\": [{\"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-authority38-20261012\\\\claude-341\\\\native\\\\work\\\\r10-create-wo.evidence\\\\2-document-1.md\", \"sha256\": \"b3eec973d463bc01d046630000796646249d3c4677844e1fcc10f0964666e738\", \"bytes\": 4367, \"text\": \"+++\\nid = \\\"WO-P3-001\\\"\\ntype = \\\"work_order\\\"\\ntitle = \\\"<Bounded implementation objective>\\\"\\nstatus = \\\"draft\\\"\\nowners = [\\\"<engineering owner>\\\"]\\ncreated = \\\"2026-10-12\\\"\\nupdated = \\\"2026-10-12\\\"\\n\\n[assurance]\\ncommit_bound_verification = \\\"<required or not_required>\\\"\\nrationale = \\\"<why future decisions do or do not require commit-bound assurance>\\\"\\ndecided_by = \\\"<actual human who confirmed the assurance classification>\\\"\\n\\n[execution_scope]\\npaths = [\\n  \\\"<exact/repository-relative/path>\\\",\\n  \\\"<repository-relative/component-prefix/>\\\",\\n]\\n\\n[relations]\\nimplements = [\\\"REQ-xxx\\\"]\\nspecifications = [\\\"SPEC-xxx\\\"]\\nverification = [\\\"VER-xxx\\\"]\\n+++\\n\\n# Work Order: <title>\\n\\nBefore approval, apply the shared design principle and `work_order` checklist in\\n`docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md`.\\n\\n## Lifecycle\\n\\nUse `approved` to authorize bounded execution and `implemented` after the work and retained evidence are complete. Governance-only work normally stops at `implemented`. Use `verified` or `released` only when an eligible commit-bound VREC explicitly covers this work order under the repository's configured provenance policy.\\n\\nBefore approval, classify commit-bound verification explicitly. Use `required` when future engineering, assurance, operational, or release decisions will rely on the correctness of changed executable behavior, managed policy, CI, definitions, traceability, or other trusted engineering state. Use `not_required` only when the work solely records or transports an already authorized verification, release, supersession, publication, or deployment decision. Split mixed scope or use `required`; uncertainty requires escalation rather than an inferred default.\\n\\nBefore approval, replace every `[execution_scope].paths` placeholder. An entry\\nending in `/` admits that directory and its descendants. Every other entry\\nadmits one exact repository-relative path. Do not use absolute paths,\\nbackslashes, wildcards, dot components, drive prefixes, URIs, or duplicate\\ncase variants.\\n\\nApproval authorizes routine execution through the single procedure in\\n`docs/engineering/harness/AUTHORITY.md#authority-from-work-approval`. The selected person or\\nagent can start, implement, check, record completion and prepare required\\nverification within this scope without separate permission for those steps.\\nRecord completion only after the work and evidence are complete. Owner\\nacceptance and delivery remain distinct. Specify any authorized push/PR action\\nand destination in the decision envelope; do not imply merge or publication.\\n\\nAdd `architecture = [\\\"ARCH-xxx\\\", \\\"ADR-xxx\\\"]` under `[relations]` when architecture applies. The relation selects every applicable architecture plus every required deciding ADR. An ADR may be omitted only for a selected architecture whose accepted `decision_assessment` is `no_significant_decision`; every `adr_required` architecture needs at least one selected active ADR that decides it.\\n\\nAn architecture is applicable when it addresses an architecturally significant requirement implemented by this work order. Every selected architecture must conform to at least one of the selected specifications. Omit the `architecture` relation only when no active architecture addresses any implemented requirement. Routine requirements without an active `addresses` edge do not require fabricated architecture coverage. A present `architecture` relation must not be empty.\\n\\n## Objective\\n\\n## In scope\\n\\n## Out of scope\\n\\n## Authorized decision envelope\\n\\nWhat may the implementation agent decide locally?\\n\\n## Constraints\\n\\n## Expected change surface\\n\\nInspect the affected implementation and supporting paths before approval,\\nusing the selected released DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md procedure. A partial response cannot support that claim.\n26\tIf the host saves or truncates a response, inspect the returned file through an\n27\tavailable, permitted read tool.
