  "content": "1\t# Hosted artifact graph: sandbox protocol and implementation\n2\t\n3\tThis guide explains the approved SPEC-HAG-001/002/003 protocol. The private\n4\tservice and candidate remote client are implemented under WO-HAG-001. Development\n5\tqualification is retained in VREC-HAG-004. Use [the operating procedure](../../server/README.md) to build and test\n6\tthe exact combination. Git remains authoritative. This is not a public service.\n7\t\n8\tDEC-HAG-001 is decided. The bounded revision in DEC-HAG-002 selects public\n9\tevaluator 0.22.1 for this sandbox; candidate client 0.22.2 is separate.\n10\t\n11\tPhase 3 adds an explicit private test-copy lifecycle pilot under WO-HAG-008 and\n12\tSPEC-HAG-007. Git remains authoritative. The pilot uses separately versioned\n13\t[closed operations](../../server/contracts/lifecycle-v2.md), exact multi-file\n14\tsnapshots and ordinary disposable Git history. Its eight required VER-HAG-006\n15\tscenarios passed, and mmzen verified\n16\t[VREC-HAG-005](../engineering/hosted-artifact-graph/verification-records/VREC-HAG-005.md).\n17\tPR #542 integrated that result. The separate native Codex/Claude qualification is\n18\t[in progress under WO-HAG-009](../engineering/hosted-artifact-graph/evidence/WO-HAG-009/report.md).\n19\tAuthentication and database ACL implementation remain deferred. The existing\n20\tprivate access controls and RISK-HAG-001 remain in place.\n21\t\n22\t## Project and source identities\n23\t\n24\tThe explicit operator initialization command allocates a UUIDv4 `project_id`\n25\tonce and stores it on Project with `command_version = 0` and `schema_revision = 1`.\n26\tAn operator may supply a fixed UUID for a reproducible disposable test. Repeating\n27\tinitialization confirms the same identity; it cannot silently replace a store.\n28\tThe readable name `hosted-artifact-poc` is a display name, outside revision hashes.\n29\tClients obtain the UUID from initialization output or authenticated status.\n30\tRequests never allocate projects and never take a principal identity on trust.\n31\t\n32\tThe reference source is `https://github.com/mmzen/se_harness`, Git SHA-1 commit\n33\t`82f5a0ed7438a73da8f03aee23ad02a9b8cc09e1`. The source manifest lists every formal\n34\tartifact path, ID, blob OID, byte length and raw SHA-256. Generate it from Git\n35\tblobs using the released discovery list, not Explorer output or a worktree read.\n36\tOn Windows, `git archive` needs `-c core.autocrlf=false`: archive defaults can\n37\tconvert LF to CRLF even when the blob is LF. Compare each exported artifact with\n38\t`git cat-file` bytes. Preserve original bytes, including CRLF when present in Git.\n39\t\n40\tThe operator mounts the pinned source/evidence snapshot read-only. Import requests\n41\tidentify this configured source; they cannot make the server fetch URLs or read\n42\tarbitrary host paths. Check every manifest entry against its mounted bytes, then\n43\tuse the released parser for the canonical artifact set. Missing, duplicate,\n44\textra or conflicting entries prevent complete import. Supporting code/evidence\n45\tbelongs to the immutable historical resolver, outside the 64 MiB artifact limit.\n46\t\n47\t## Revision and baseline bytes\n48\t\n49\t`server/contracts/remote-v1.json` names the exact fields. A StoredRevision contains\n50\t`revision_id`, `envelope` and `document_base64`. The envelope binds the full raw\n51\tdocument SHA-256; the store verifies it against decoded bytes. Imported provenance\n52\tcontains the original repository/object format/commit and Git blob OID. Draft\n53\tprovenance records the authenticated principal, operation key and server-assigned\n54\tcreation time once. An accepted retry returns the retained bytes and time.\n55\t\n56\tCanonical JSON uses UTF-8 without BOM, sorted object keys, compact separators,\n57\tliteral Unicode, no Unicode normalization and no floats. Reject duplicate object\n58\tkeys and non-finite numbers before decoding into a map. Semantic sets are sorted\n59\tand deduplicated before encoding: relation targets, artifact selection keys and\n60\tresolved relation triples. Preserve meaningful sequence order. Raw document\n61\tbytes receive no line-ending or Unicode conversion for the new revision scheme.\n62\t\n63\tThe revision hash input is `se-harness-artifact-revision/v1`, LF, then canonical\n64\tenvelope bytes. `revision_id` is `sha256:` plus the lowercase hex digest. The\n65\tbaseline hash input uses `se-harness-artifact-baseline/v1`, LF and the canonical\n66\tmanifest; its identifier is that scheme plus `:sha256:` and the hex digest.\n67\tNeither hash payload includes its own derived ID or a future verification record.\n68\tThe baseline manifest binds sorted selection, resolved revision-to-revision\n69\ttriples, provenance and the exact evaluator identity. A missing relation target\n70\tprevents a complete baseline. Saved incomplete drafts remain distinct from\n71\tcomplete frozen selections and from approved definitions.\n72\t\n73\tThe fixture file `canonical-v1.json` supplies exact JSON strings and hash-input\n74\tbytes for three connected draft artifacts, LF/CRLF, composed/decomposed Unicode,\n75\ta body edit, sequence order and a baseline. These are synthetic byte examples,\n76\tnot claimed receipts. Its all-zero parent baseline is an explicit fixture marker.\n77\tFuture serializer tests must consume these fixed expectations; do not regenerate\n78\texpected hashes from serializer output.\n79\t\n80\t## Command-specific fields\n81\t\n82\tEvery mutation carries the project ID, operation key, expected project version,\n83\texact evaluator archive/payload identity, and candidate client version/wheel hash.\n84\tThe server checks compatibility against the qualified tuple before mutation.\n85\tThe authenticated principal comes from credentials; claimed component identity\n86\tdoes not grant authority. Reject unknown fields and contradictory selectors.\n87\t\n88\t| Operation | Required selection and content | Accepted effect |\n89\t| --- | --- | --- |\n90\t| `import` | Complete configured source manifest; no baseline/context selector | Original immutable revisions and imported baseline, deterministic report, receipt, one project-version increment |\n91\t| `draft-open` | Base baseline ID and selected work-order ID | New UUID context at version 0; unchanged base; receipt and project-version increment |\n92\t| `create-artifact` | Context ID/version, domain, supported type; ID or explicit null for allocation | New draft revision and proposed selection; context and project versions increment once |\n93\t| `revise-artifact` | Context ID/version, artifact ID, expected selected revision, complete base64 document | New draft revision and derived relations; replace only that context selection; increment both versions |\n94\t| `freeze` | Context ID/version | New immutable baseline from the resolved complete view; project version increments; context stays open with its original base and version |\n95\t\n96\tAn empty project has no baseline to select. Import therefore has no view selector.\n97\tOperation lookup is `GET /v1/projects/{project}/operations/{key}` and is scoped to\n98\tthe authenticated principal. It requires neither a baseline nor a current version.\n99\tReadiness also has no view selector. These are the applicable-field interpretations\n100\tof HAG-API-001/002, not changes to the named protocol schemes.\n101\t\n102\tThe draft view is its base plus explicit proposals. New IDs are reserved across\n103\tall retained project artifacts, not just the selected baseline. The command adapter\n104\tuses the released authoring template and allocation behavior in a disposable\n105\tprojection. The isolated public 0.22.1 evaluator supplies standalone draft\n106\tadmission. Protected lifecycle/provenance/evidence fields remain protected.\n107\t\n108\tFreeze does not rebase, close, clear or otherwise change its context. Editing that\n109\tcontext later creates another proposed revision. Opening a fresh context from\n110\tthe frozen baseline explicitly selects a new base. Repeating an already accepted\n111\toperation key returns its original receipt even if live versions have advanced.\n112\tA different request under the same principal/project/key returns conflict.\n113\t\n114\t## Atomic acceptance and reads\n115\t\n116\tBind evaluation to project version N and the exact context/revision selection.\n117\tIn a short explicit database transaction, conditionally change the same Project\n118\tguard from N to N+1 and recheck context/revision guards. Commit the new content,\n119\tderived edges, selection changes and accepted receipt in that transaction. A\n120\tcompeting guard update or constraint conflict refuses; a process mutex is not\n121\tthe transaction boundary. Do not retry against newer input without caller action.\n122\t\n123\tImported content and repeated source identity cannot disagree. Reimporting the\n124\tsame complete source may return the already present immutable baseline; it must\n125\tnot duplicate revisions or change the stable import report. A new operation key\n126\tstill has its own guarded command/receipt; retrying the original key has no new\n127\teffect. A refusal creates no accepted receipt.\n128\t\n129\tDomain reads select either an immutable baseline or an explicit context/version.\n130\tExact revision, work context, comparison, impact, verification lineage and check\n131\tall return selection identity, provenance, evaluator identity and completeness.\n132\tComparison explicitly names both views. Check is read-only and checkpoint-free;\n133\tit reports released findings and cannot apply a lifecycle transition. Operation\n134\tlookup returns the recorded receipt, not a recomputed result at the latest view.\n135\tUnsupported live-view forms are refused until their semantics are qualified.\n136\t\n137\tDomain and MCP reads share handlers. Context completeness requires all selected\n138\tgoverning artifacts and declared paths; truncated results cannot establish a\n139\tgoverning claim. The pinned reference fixture has 12 governing IDs and 11 paths\n140\tfor WO-RLS-038; VREC dependencies remain separately classified.\n141\t\n142\t## Validation at each stage\n143\t\n144\t| Stage | Required assessment | Meaning of success |\n145\t| --- | --- | --- |\n146\t| Decode | Strict UTF-8, unique JSON keys, integer-only request numbers, closed wire shape, contained source paths and configured source identity | A request can be evaluated; no authority or mutation follows from decoding |\n147\t| Import | Complete Git manifest, raw byte/provenance equality, released parsing and relation validation, all referenced targets resolved | A faithful historical baseline; imported lifecycle claims acquire no new decision event |\n148\t| Draft creation/revision | Released standalone draft classification, server rights, protected-field comparison and exact input selection | A saved draft may retain the evaluator's narrow permitted incompleteness; malformed content or supplied invalid relations refuse |\n149\t| Freeze | Complete selection, resolvable declared edges, canonical manifest and version guards | An immutable baseline of that selection; draft members remain drafts |\n150\t| Context/read/check | Explicit view, exact released context/findings, original-path historical resolver and honest resource limits | A bounded observation; `complete` does not mean approved or gate-passing |\n151\t| Approval/verification/release | Unsupported in this sandbox | No remote command can confer a human decision or produce a verified/released record |\n152\t\n153\tThe draft stage uses the selected public 0.22.1 evaluator. Its archive and payload\n154\tare fixed by SPEC-HAG-003. Earlier Phase 1 reports retain their original 0.22.0\n155\tobservations and unresolved-dependency status as historical evidence.\n156\t\n157\t## Read request and response details\n158\t\n159\t`server/contracts/read-v1.json` defines the seven POST request variants and their\n160\tresponse shapes. HTTP path operation/project and body values must agree. MCP\n161\tuses the same normalized request and authenticated handler. A context selector\n162\trequires its exact version. If that version has changed, refuse with 409; no\n163\timplicit latest view or historical context reconstruction is promised. Freeze a\n164\tselection when later reads need an immutable identity. A compare request names\n165\ttwo views in the same project.\n166\t\n167\t| Read | Selection and returned data |\n168\t| --- | --- |\n169\t| `revision` | Artifact ID and revision ID must match the selected view. Return the complete StoredRevision, including raw bytes; a known revision outside that view is an unknown selected identity |\n170\t| `work-context` | Selected work order, governing bindings, exact declared paths, dependencies and relevant decisions in separate fields; embed the unchanged checkpoint-free evaluator result |\n171\t| `compare` | Added/removed/changed artifact bindings plus added/removed resolved relation triples; a missing side of a binding is null |\n172\t| `impact` | The root and reverse reachability through known declared relations at the selected view, within depth; exclude the root from the returned artifact list |\n173\t| `lineage` | The root and connected declared verification/evidence relations, with endpoint revisions; this reports recorded links and claims, not new assurance |\n174\t| `check` | Only a WO, VREC, RLS or DEC accepted by the selected released checkpoint-free `check`; return its JSON unchanged with the selected binding |\n175\t| `cypher` | Columns and rows from the admitted query, bound to the selected view; column names and row widths agree and graph values expose only the documented public properties |\n176\t\n177\tSort domain bindings by artifact ID, paths lexically, and edges by\n178\t`(source.artifact_id, kind, target.artifact_id)`. Preserve evaluator output and\n179\tCypher row order. A read's `provenance` names the unique Git sources of its selected\n180\tinputs; synthetic draft-only examples may have none. Draft provenance is retained\n181\tin each revision envelope. Resolve every returned revision through the view.\n182\tFor comparison, resolve each side independently. These equality and resolution\n183\tchecks require the service; JSON Schema checks only their shapes.\n184\t\n185\t`complete=true` means that this response contains the whole requested answer at\n186\tthe stated view and no unresolved references. It can still contain draft hints,\n187\topen decisions, failed gates or a missing historical binding in the unchanged\n188\tevaluator result. When missing bindings prevent the requested assessment, report\n189\t`complete=false` with `binding_unavailable` and identify the exact missing input\n190\tin evaluator output. Do not convert `not_assessable` into a pass.\n191\t\n192\tBudgets apply to the aggregate graph rows across all returned collections, not\n193\t500 rows per nested array. Paths and unresolved-reference entries also consume\n194\tthat allowance. The byte budget includes the entire serialized response and\n195\tembedded evaluator output; never truncate that JSON. Traversal stops must be\n196\tvisible even if the last returned node has no displayed outgoing edge. A bounded\n197\tpartial response uses `complete=false`, the specific limit and concrete\n198\tinstructions to repeat at the same view with a narrower selection. That strategy\n199\tdoes not turn a partial work context into a governing claim. If the operation\n200\thas no meaningful narrower selection, repeat with a larger budget within the\n201\tfixed limits; if the minimum honest response cannot fit, refuse with 429.\n202\tUnresolved graph targets require a new complete view, not silent continuation at\n203\tan altered context version. No pagination cursor or server-side read session is\n204\tintroduced in this initial contract.\n205\t\n206\tThe baseline GET retrieves one canonical manifest resource, not a row query:\n207\t`{schema: \"se-harness-artifact-baseline/v1\", baseline_id, manifest}`. Validate the\n208\tmanifest with `BaselineManifest`, recompute its identity, and return the full\n209\tselection and resolved relations. The 2 MiB response limit still applies; reject\n210\tan import/freeze whose complete baseline response cannot fit before committing.\n211\tDo not clip the manifest to the graph-query row budget or label a fragment as B.\n212\tOperation GET returns exactly the stored accepted `remote-result/v1` object for\n213\tthe authenticated principal/project/key. Status GET reports project UUID, schema\n214\trevision, readiness, actual evaluator and component identities, and the supported\n215\tprotocols. Missing built/qualified identities keep readiness false. Neither GET\n216\trequires a context selector; operation lookup never guesses an acceptance.\n217\t\n218\t## Accepted receipts, refusals and retry order\n219\t\n220\t`server/contracts/result-v1.json` defines immutable accepted receipts and stable\n221\tremote refusal codes. Every successful mutation returns HTTP 200 and one UUIDv4\n222\t`receipt_id`. Store that entire result with the operation. Bind `request_digest`\n223\tto SHA-256 of `se-harness-remote-command/v1`, one LF, and canonical JSON of the\n224\tcomplete validated command. Canonicalize manifest entries as their declared set\n225\tbefore hashing; retain original document bytes and all expected versions. Reject\n226\tduplicate manifest entries before normalization. Credentials, connection metadata\n227\tand transport timestamps are outside the command and digest.\n228\t\n229\tAn accepted result's project `after` equals `before + 1`. For context open,\n230\tcontext `before` is null and `after` is zero. Create/revise increments the context\n231\tonce; freeze reports equal context versions. Import has a null context version\n232\trecord. The returned view names the imported/frozen baseline or resulting context;\n233\taffected revision IDs are sorted unique IDs created by this operation, so an\n234\tidentical-source reimport may report none. Receipt IDs are correlation identities,\n235\tnot substitutes for revision or baseline hashes.\n236\t`affected_artifacts` supplies the corresponding artifact/revision bindings, so\n237\tcreation with automatic ID allocation returns the allocated artifact ID directly.\n238\tIts revision set must equal `affected_revision_ids`; a refusal returns both empty.\n239\t\n240\tApply checks in this order:\n241\t\n242\t1. Authenticate and enforce project/operation access, then perform bounded strict\n243\t   decoding and shape checks. Reject unknown fields such as caller-supplied actor.\n244\t2. For a valid command, compute its request digest and inspect the accepted key.\n245\t   Equal digest returns the original result immediately, even after versions or\n246\t   the configured tuple change. Different digest returns key-reuse conflict.\n247\t   Current project access remains required; never evaluate a retry as a new write.\n248\t3. For a new key, check the supported tuple, known selections, then project,\n249\t   context and selected-revision guards in that order. Refuse the first mismatch.\n250\t4. Check source/protected input and invoke the released evaluator against the\n251\t   bound selection. Invalid draft/import, incomplete freeze and unavailable\n252\t   mandatory bindings have distinct 422 codes. Resource refusal is 429.\n253\t5. Recheck guards and selection in the write transaction, write all effects and\n254\t   receipt, then commit. On a concurrent equal-key conflict, read the winner's\n255\t   committed receipt. Never update a stale request's expectations automatically.\n256\t\n257\tMalformed input uses 400, unauthenticated 401, forbidden 403, unknown accessible\n258\tidentity 404, stale/key/source/tuple conflict 409, invalid content 422 and resource\n259\trefusal 429. The schema enumerates the `HAG_REMOTE_*` codes. These codes wrap the\n260\tremote operation; existing evaluator codes and JSON remain unchanged. Refusals\n261\thave no receipt and no affected revisions. Populate correlation/view/versions\n262\tonly when known and authorized; otherwise use null. When versions are returned\n263\ton refusal, before equals after. Read refusals use null command correlation and\n264\tversions. Denial must not leak another project's identities or findings.\n265\t\n266\tThe schemas describe the selected evaluator profile as well as wire shapes.\n267\tTheir pinned evaluator constants are compatibility checks: a well-formed but\n268\tdifferent version/archive/payload is 409, not malformed JSON. Keep those checks\n269\tseparate from primitive shape decoding and accepted-key recovery. Strictly\n270\tdecode base64 and require its canonical encoding before digest calculation;\n271\tinvalid base64 is malformed input, while invalid decoded TOML is invalid draft\n272\tcontent. A successful shape check alone never supplies an admission decision.\n273\t\n274\tA lost response, evaluator process crash or ambiguous database commit is not an\n275\taccepted or refused receipt. The client reports transport uncertainty and uses\n276\toperation lookup or an identical retry. A 404 lookup alone cannot prove that a\n277\tconcurrent transaction rolled back. A known precommit failure leaves no effects;\n278\tan unknown commit must be reconciled against the authoritative operation store.\n279\t\n280\t## Scenario and schema qualification\n281\t\n282\t`tests/hosted_artifact_graph/fixtures/scenarios-v1.json` maps all twelve\n283\tVER-HAG-001 cases to explicit positive/failure outcomes and storage support. Every\n284\thosted execution entry is `unperformed`. Its wire examples contain synthetic\n285\tidentities and demonstrate shape only; their apparent receipt values are not\n286\tobserved acceptances. The original fixed canonical vectors remain unchanged.\n287\t\n288\tFor independent shape qualification, install `jsonschema==4.26.0` in a disposable\n289\tenvironment and run its Python against:\n290\t\n291\t```text\n292\tpython -I -B tests/hosted_artifact_graph/validate_wire_contracts.py\n293\tpython -m unittest tests.test_hosted_artifact_graph\n294\t```\n295\t\n296\tThe first command registers the three local schemas without network resolution,\n297\tvalidates fixed vectors and the complete source manifest, and exercises accepted\n298\tand rejected shapes. It does not introduce a core runtime dependency. The second\n299\tcommand checks fixed bytes and digests through the existing test entry point.\n300\tNeither command executes service admission, transactions, Cypher or recovery.\n301\t\n302\t## Physical representation\n303\t\n304\tProject stores identity, schema revision and the integer command guard. Artifact\n305\tstores project-scoped identity. Revision stores its canonical `envelope_json`,\n306\t`document_base64`, artifact ID, raw document digest and derived type/status.\n307\tBaseline stores `manifest_json`; its SELECTS edges are derived from that manifest.\n308\tDraftContext stores base baseline, selected work order, version and proposals.\n309\tOperation stores project/principal/key, semantic request digest and immutable\n310\t`result_json`. JSON text properties retain exact machine structures without\n311\texpanding scalar fields into graph nodes.\n312\t\n313\tHAS_REVISION links Artifact to Revision. SELECTS links Baseline to Revision.\n314\tBASED_ON links DraftContext to Baseline. PROPOSES links DraftContext to Revision\n315\twith the selected artifact ID. DECLARES links Revision to the target Artifact,\n316\twith `kind` and `target_artifact_id`; resolve the target revision through the view.\n317\tCross-project edges, duplicate selections, and edges inconsistent with canonical\n318\tcontent fail import/export checks. Unresolved authoring placeholders remain\n319\texplicit findings and cannot become invented valid target nodes.\n320\t\n321\tThe public Cypher surface uses the selected project's Artifact and Revision\n322\tnodes, its selected Baseline (or context/base), and the corresponding storage\n323\tedges above. Project exposes only project ID, schema revision and the observed\n324\tcommand version. Artifact exposes project/artifact IDs. Revision exposes its IDs,\n325\ttype, status, document digest, envelope JSON and document base64. Baseline exposes\n326\tits IDs and manifest JSON. DraftContext exposes its IDs, selected work order,\n327\tbase baseline and context version. DECLARES exposes kind and target artifact ID;\n328\tPROPOSES exposes the selected artifact ID. Other edge properties are not public.\n329\t\n330\tOperation nodes and their result JSON are private to command handling and the\n331\tprincipal-scoped operation lookup. They are not available through Cypher. A\n332\tquery cannot use internal labels/properties, an unselected revision, another\n333\tbaseline/context, or caller-authored project filters to widen this surface. The\n334\tservice must apply that projection before query execution and use the same\n335\tread-only transaction snapshot for selection and results. This is application\n336\tadmission; it does not resolve RISK-HAG-001 or prove database privileges.\n337\t\n338\t`graph-v1.cypher` supplies six composite/single uniqueness keys, key/content\n339\texistence constraints and explicit lookup indexes. Memgraph requires separate\n340\tindexes for uniqueness constraints ([official constraint documentation](https://memgraph.com/docs/fundamentals/constraints)).\n341\tSchema creation must be an explicit operator step on an empty store; later\n342\tstartup verifies it and refuses mismatch. Explicit initialization, stored revision\n343\tmismatch and missing-index refusal have now been tested for the exact candidate\n344\tin the [qualification report](../engineering/hosted-artifact-graph/evidence/WO-HAG-001/phase2-20261005/protocol04-assessment.md).\n345\t\n346\t## Deferred boundary and historical qualification\n347\t\n348\tCypher remains part of the sandbox: parse a narrow read grammar, constrain the\n349\tselected view, apply row/byte/depth/time limits and roll back the read transaction.\n350\tDatabase-enforced read-only access remains RISK-HAG-001; no authoritative-pilot or\n351\tpublic-service claim is made. The current contract does not authorize a parser\n352\tbypass, a replacement lifecycle policy or suppression of malformed input.\n353\t\n354\tThe candidate now includes its dependency lock, image/Compose files, admission\n355\tadapter, transaction implementation, remote client and plugin guidance. The\n356\tpackaged-client walkthrough, live concurrency and refusal checks, independent\n357\tevaluator comparison and restart/restore have passed for candidate\n358\t`7290af984337e122b193981a187e58c29de41a16`. The linked report preserves earlier\n359\tfailures and exact component identities.\n360\t\n361\tThe [later Claude qualification](../engineering/hosted-artifact-graph/evidence/WO-HAG-001/claude-20261006/report.md)\n362\tuses candidate `5fa50787dba1fdd010f625335d844416dd3bf002`. Authentication works;\n363\tthe corrected MCP schemas allow all seven native tools. The guided CLI sequence,\n364\tHTTP/MCP response comparison and repeated live boundary suite pass. Claude still\n365\tmisreported a large partial response as complete. A smaller follow-up was correctly\n366\treported; it does not erase that failure.\n367\t\n368\tThe [Codex continuation](../engineering/hosted-artifact-graph/evidence/WO-HAG-001/codex-20261006/report.md)\n369\ton that same candidate completes 18 installed-client operations and seven native\n370\tMCP reads, with exact HTTP parity and correct partial-result reporting. It retains\n371\tthe original wheel-access and MCP approval-policy failures. The successful runs\n372\tuse the normal host profile and approval reviewer; no persistent permissions were\n373\tweakened. Exact candidate guidance was read explicitly, so automatic plugin loading\n374\tand desktop remain unobserved. Earlier restart/restore and evaluator results above\n375\tremain historical candidate04 evidence. WO-HAG-001 remains in_progress, and PR #535\n376\tremains draft.\n377\t\n378\t\n379\t## Current Phase 2 qualification\n380\t\n381\tThe [current assessment](../engineering/hosted-artifact-graph/evidence/WO-HAG-001/final-20261006/report.md)\n382\trecords all twelve required scenarios for the final candidate, its exact component\n383\ttuple, real native CLI observations and restart/restore. It preserves the earlier\n384\tfailures above. Large saved MCP responses expose their completeness metadata to\n385\tbounded file reads. Parser failures preserve their errors without returning an\n386\tunusable catalog that exceeds the CLI response bound.\n387\t\n388\tThe assessment distinguishes tested behavior from human verification. Read the\n389\tlinked work orders and verification record for current lifecycle state. Codex\n390\tcandidate guidance was read explicitly; automatic loading and desktop were not\n391\ttested. Database-side read-only enforcement remains RISK-HAG-001. Phase 3 pilot\n392\tauthority and Phase 4 production/release obligations require later work.\n393\t\n394\t## Authoring cost and candidate guidance\n395\t\n396\tWO-HAG-011 adds typed file inputs and compact evidence-backed results to the\n397\texisting client. See [the concrete interface](harnessctl-reference.md#concise-hosted-authoring).\n398\tSkills route hosted drafting, lifecycle rehearsal and repository work separately.\n399\tThese candidate changes require their own tests; earlier service qualification\n400\tdoes not establish improved task duration or context use. Git remains authoritative.\n401\t"

  "content": "1\t# Private lifecycle rehearsal wire contract\n2\t\n3\tThe v2 endpoint accepts only `lifecycle-v2.json`. Every request and result selects\n4\ttest data. Git remains authoritative for real work. Existing v1 routes and the\n5\tseven read-only MCP tools keep their contract; they cannot dispatch v2 actions.\n6\tIn an explicitly configured test project their results use\n7\t`se-harness-graph-read/v2`, with a required test-copy label. Ordinary Phase 2\n8\tprojects retain their v1 read result shapes. Revision byte hashing is unchanged;\n9\ttest revisions add explicit test-copy provenance, without rewriting v1 fixtures.\n10\t\n11\t`inspect` accepts check, preflight and validate. `preview` runs a mutation in a\n12\tdiscarded projection and returns its input digest and complete proposed file\n13\teffects. `apply` requires that digest, reruns the released checks, and commits\n14\tthe complete result once. A preview never changes graph state. Actor strings\n15\tare synthetic test inputs, not authenticated human consent.\n16\t\n17\tThe digest binds project/context versions, all selected revision IDs, retained\n18\tsnapshot, exact command/file inputs, principal and released evaluator identity.\n19\tMode and operation key are excluded from the preview binding, but included in\n20\tthe accepted request digest used for principal-scoped retry lookup.\n21\t\n22\t## Permitted file effects\n23\t\n24\t| Action | Permitted effects in the disposable projection |\n25\t| --- | --- |\n26\t| check / preflight / validate | No file writes |\n27\t| transition | Only selected newly authored artifacts and evaluator sidecars declared by their records |\n28\t| decide | Selected new decision and its explicitly linked rehearsal risk |\n29\t| raise-risk | New risk and optional paired decision at released domain paths |\n30\t| handoff | Declared bounded file inputs within the selected work order's scope; released handoff packet and evaluator evidence |\n31\t| capture-verification | New VREC and its released evaluator sidecar |\n32\t| prepare-release | New RLS and its released evaluator sidecar |\n33\t\n34\tHandoff first uses the released procedure's fixed `evidence` preparation command,\n35\tthen `check --checkpoint handoff --from-git`. It exposes no arbitrary evidence\n36\tcommand. File inputs cannot alter formal records, harness selection/instructions,\n37\tGit configuration, hooks, or executables used by the service. The service never\n38\truns source tests. The local qualification runner supplies observed test evidence.\n39\t\n40\tThe adapter enumerates every regular file before and after the operation. It\n41\trejects deletions, links, unexpected paths, missing sidecars, imported-record\n42\tchanges and evaluator failure before opening an acceptance transaction.\n43\t\n44\t## Snapshots and Git\n45\t\n46\tEach accepted operation retains an immutable complete snapshot: exact file bytes,\n47\tself-contained Git history and its source/test-candidate identities. Content\n48\tdigests bind the snapshot to the resulting baseline. Context heads, artifact\n49\trevisions, snapshot and receipt share one database transaction. Temporary Git\n50\trepositories are discarded after the result is accepted or refused.\n51\t\n52\tCapture first commits the exact input projection as test candidate P. P excludes\n53\tthe new VREC. The output retains the input hosted baseline B, source fixture S,\n54\tP and evaluator identity. Actual implementation candidate C is recorded separately\n55\tby qualification. Subsequent commits never alter the candidate stored in a VREC.\n56\t\n57\tExport selects an immutable baseline, checks its complete snapshot and Git bundle,\n58\tand returns exact bounded bytes. The installed client writes only to a new explicit\n59\tdestination and leaves an incomplete marker until every file hash is checked.\n60\tNo export writes to or synchronizes an authoritative repository.\n61\t\n62\t## Bounds and failure categories\n63\t\n64\tKeep 4 MiB requests, 2 MiB responses, 1 MiB individual file inputs, two concurrent\n65\tprojections, 120-second evaluator calls and 10,000 retained artifact revisions.\n66\tA pilot snapshot/export that exceeds the response bound is refused atomically.\n67\tRetained complete snapshots have a separate explicit project count bound of 128.\n68\tThis is a small private pilot, not an unbounded document store.\n69\t\n70\tBoundary/malformed requests, stale inputs, evaluator refusals, invalid output and\n71\tunknown transport outcomes remain distinct. Read the operation receipt after a\n72\tlost reply. A changed request under the same accepted key is a conflict. The\n73\tservice does not automatically rerun a mutation at a newer version.\n74\t\n75\t## Bounded Cypher reads\n76\t\n77\tThe existing `cypher` MCP/HTTP read selects one exact view. Its request uses the\n78\tread schema, not the lifecycle command schema. Read the public labels and\n79\tproperties in [the physical representation](../../docs/notes/hosted-artifact-graph.md#physical-representation)\n80\tbefore selecting a query. `Artifact` and `Revision` are distinct public labels.\n81\t\n82\tThe admitted form has one `MATCH` or `OPTIONAL MATCH` path, labelled nodes,\n83\toptional parameterized property comparisons, `RETURN`, and a numeric `LIMIT`.\n84\t\n85\tRelationship paths are supported. Use `-[edge:TYPE]->` or `<-[edge:TYPE]-` between\n86\tlabelled nodes, with a public relationship type from the physical representation.\n87\tEach node and relationship needs a distinct variable name. Anonymous relationship\n88\tsyntax such as `-[:TYPE]->` is refused; a refusal of that form does not mean\n89\trelationship traversal is unsupported. Return-column aliases (`AS alias`) are\n90\toptional.\n91\t\n92\tFor example, with `parameters` containing a selected artifact ID under `artifact`:\n93\t\n94\t```cypher\n95\tMATCH (r:Revision) WHERE r.artifact_id = $artifact RETURN r.artifact_id AS id, r.status AS status LIMIT 10\n96\t```\n97\t\n98\tUse the response's completeness fields with the requested row/byte/depth budget.\n99\tThis example supplies no project filter: the service binds the selected project\n100\tand view. Unlabelled nodes, `WITH`, aggregation and arbitrary database syntax are\n101\tnot part of this small public read form. A rejected query has not completed a read.\n102\tKeep the exact refusal; consult the documented form before constructing another\n103\trequest. Do not weaken query admission or bypass it through a direct database call.\n104\t"
}

{
  "tool_use_id": "toolu_012Vvh22NrK4hgX9RruuadZA",
  "type": "tool_result",
  "content": "{\"resources\": [{\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/AUTHORIZE_WORK.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-authority38-20261012\\\\claude-341\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\AUTHORIZE_WORK.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"991b526d6280554dcca6901c1790e5519b8894e08635a1f10618aa84a29aaf28\", \"bytes\": 15212, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"read-this-when\", \"title\": \"Read this when\", \"anchor\": \"read-this-when\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 6, \"content\": \"## Read this when\\n\\nThe proposed package needs validation, review or a human authorization decision.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"8b18d925f39f314acc9ea6bcc9820015c4c9430d836144e85bc31b670752371b\"}, {\"heading\": \"before-this-action\", \"title\": \"Before this action\", \"anchor\": \"before-this-action\", \"start_line\": 7, \"end_line\": 10, \"content\": \"## Before this action\\n\\nFor reviewing each definition, use its selected [Type checklist](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#type-checklists). Before review, read [AUTHORITY.md#decision-rights](AUTHORITY.md#decision-rights) and [AUTHORITY.md#delegation-and-separation](AUTHORITY.md#delegation-and-separation). Before approving work, read [WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification). For an open or deferred decision, read [RISKS_AND_DECISIONS.md#blocking-decisions](RISKS_AND_DECISIONS.md#blocking-decisions).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"9aa32693606102a5aa74b2e4a614aed6904471e5f618dea93356baac97c86ca2\"}, {\"heading\": \"assess-the-proposed-artifact-package\", \"title\": \"Assess the proposed artifact package\", \"anchor\": \"assess-the-proposed-artifact-package\", \"start_line\": 32, \"end_line\": 83, \"content\": \"### Assess the proposed artifact package\\n\\n**Inputs:** The selected work order (WO) IDs, proposed definitions, validation\\nresults, and existing decision records (DEC).\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** None created or modified.\\n- **Transient working material:** A review list identifying the selected\\n  artifacts, their states, applicable checks, and reported blockers.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Run validation for the repository.\\n2. Inspect each selected work order (WO) with the command below.\\n3. Read the work order (WO) and its returned reading manifest.\\n4. Review the governing definitions against the relevant checklists in\\n   `docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md`.\\n5. Check that the proposed work has defined scope, verification coverage,\\n   assurance classification, and accountable humans for the required decisions.\\n6. List the blockers affecting the selected package. Keep unrelated findings\\n   separate.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl validate REPO --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint transition --target approved --json\\n```\\n\\nRead the result of each required check. A `pass` satisfies that check only;\\nit does not approve the artifacts or authorize implementation. If a required\\ncheck returns `fail` or `not_assessable`, report its identifier and stated\\nreason, then follow \\u201cIf a procedure is blocked.\\u201d Do not proceed with the\\naffected approval.\\n\\nThe last command evaluates work-order approval gates. A checkpoint-free\\n`check` only reports lifecycle context. Definition artifacts cannot be selected\\nwith `check`; their transition gates are evaluated by the previews in [Apply the selected transitions](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#apply-the-selected-transitions).\\n\\nBefore proposing a definition for approval, resolve its template placeholders.\\nIf it contains an `Open decisions` section, confirm that no unresolved decision\\nremains before setting that section to `None`. Do not remove an unresolved\\nquestion merely to make validation pass.\\n\\n**Completion:** The selected package has an explicit list of review findings\\nand approval blockers. No approval is inferred from these checks.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Resolve approval blockers](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#resolve-approval-blockers) resolves the blockers. [Obtain the human's decision](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#obtain-the-humans-decision) presents the package for a\\nhuman decision.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"2ea6b051236e13dd427317722d16ce7941ef5a4eb4789f7fdf568ab3d0cae3c0\"}, {\"heading\": \"apply-the-selected-transitions\", \"title\": \"Apply the selected transitions\", \"anchor\": \"apply-the-selected-transitions\", \"start_line\": 221, \"end_line\": 270, \"content\": \"### Apply the selected transitions\\n\\n**Inputs:** The exact artifact IDs, target states, accountable human identities,\\nreviewed file digests, decisions from [Obtain the human's decision](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#obtain-the-humans-decision), and clarification answers from\\n[Define the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#procedure) that must be retained in the transition reason.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** Only explicitly selected definitions and work orders\\n  (WO) receive the applied state and lifecycle-history entries.\\n- **Transient working material:** The preview and apply results, including\\n  any refused transition.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Confirm that the reviewed artifact contents still match the human decision.\\n2. Select only the named transitions. Approve governing definitions before\\n   dependent work, or use one explicitly selected packet for mutually\\n   dependent definitions when the evaluator permits it.\\n3. Preview the transition command below.\\n4. Compare every selected ID, target state, actor, and reported effect with\\n   the human decision. Resolve failed gates before proceeding.\\n5. Recheck reviewed definition digests immediately before applying.\\n6. Repeat the passing command with `--apply`. Do not add related artifacts\\n   that the human did not select.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set ARTIFACT-ID=TARGET --decision ARTIFACT-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set ARTIFACT-ID=TARGET --decision ARTIFACT-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\n```\\n\\nFor each clarification below the formal-decision threshold, add\\n`--reason \\\"ARTIFACT-ID=Question and confirmed answer\\\"` to both the preview\\nand apply command. Use the actual question and answer. When one artifact\\nhas several such answers, combine them into one reason value for that ID.\\nKeep each complete `ID=TEXT` value as one argument. Confirm the saved reason\\nin the resulting lifecycle entry before discarding the working notes.\\n\\nFor approval, `TARGET` is `approved`. For rejection, use `rejected` and add\\n`--reason \\\"ARTIFACT-ID=Rejection reason\\\"` to both commands. Rejection is\\nterminal. For a packet, repeat `--set` and `--decision` for every explicitly\\nselected artifact. Preview and apply must contain the same assignments.\\n\\n**Completion:** The evaluator reports the actual applied transitions, or the\\nspecific refusal. A successful preview alone changes nothing.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Confirm the authorization result](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#confirm-the-authorization-result) confirms the resulting authority and readiness.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"c5215dec9f35d1a99bf6e4d658a594a31dc544131e70db49b1b8f79514e124b8\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/AUTHORITY.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-authority38-20261012\\\\claude-341\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\AUTHORITY.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"da7202bf8d1beee631e9b4b5ba01c04b440fbf76d200ccbccbb8781a59f88431\", \"bytes\": 16163, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"decision-rights\", \"title\": \"Decision rights\", \"anchor\": \"decision-rights\", \"start_line\": 19, \"end_line\": 80, \"content\": \"## Decision rights\\n\\nA profile describes who performs an action. A decision right describes which\\naction that person or agent is authorized to perform. Being a Human does not\\ngrant every right in this table. Being an Agent grants none by itself.\\n\\nUse the artifact's recorded ownership and any existing explicit delegation to\\nidentify the accountable human. Resolve a team name or ownership label to the\\nhuman making this decision. A proposed `owners` value, an example actor name,\\nor authorship of a draft MUST NOT assign decision authority.\\n\\nApproval, verification acceptance, and release decisions MUST be made by an\\nauthorized human. An agent MAY prepare the material and apply that human's\\nrecorded decision when authorized to do so. Applying the decision does not\\nmake the agent its author. The selected IDs, reviewed content, target states,\\nand reason MUST still match the decision at apply time.\\n\\nThe following stable IDs name rights and operations, not additional profiles.\\nAll applicable gates remain required.\\n\\nThese DR-* identifiers come from the selected evaluator's decision-right\\ncatalogue. Use the right and exact action returned by that evaluator. Legacy\\nowner labels identify recorded accountabilities; they do not create additional\\nHuman/Agent profiles or assign a decision to a person by themselves.\\n\\n| Right ID                   | Accountable profile and authority                                                                                                  | Required input                                                                                                    | Result                                                                                               |\\n|----------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\\n| `DR-DEFINITION-DECIDE`     | Human authorized to decide the selected definition.                                                                                | Complete definition, exact proposed state, and required checks.                                                   | Decision on that definition only.                                                                    |\\n| `DR-WO-SELECT`             | Human authorized to approve the work scope.                                                                                        | Complete governing chain, bounded work order, and assurance classification.                                       | Approval or rejection of the selected work order.                                                    |\\n| `DR-WO-START`              | Human or Agent executing the selected work under its recorded approval.                                                            | Unchanged approved scope and passing start checks.                                                                | The selected work order becomes `in_progress`.                                                       |\\n| `DR-WO-COMPLETE`           | Human or Agent executing the selected work under its recorded approval.                                                            | Completed scope, passing handoff checks, and retained evidence.                                                   | The selected work order becomes `implemented`.                                                       |\\n| `DR-VREC-PREPARE`          | Human or Agent under the approval of every selected work order.                                                                    | Exact candidate, selected work and verification contracts, retained evidence, and passing preparation checks.     | One `ready` verification record; no acceptance decision.                                             |\\n| `DR-VREC-DECIDE`           | Human authorized to accept verification for the selected candidate.                                                                | Ready record, assessed evidence, and the exact verify, reject, or supersede decision.                             | Decision on that verification record only.                                                           |\\n| `DR-DELIVERY-SELECT`       | Human authorized for the chosen integration or release-preparation path.                                                           | Exact candidate, destination, and coverage required by that path.                                                 | One path-specific instruction.                                                                       |\\n| `DR-RLS-PREPARE`           | Human authorizes the release inputs; a Human or Agent may carry out the preparation.                                               | Exact release contract, candidate, included work, verification coverage, version, and passing preparation checks. | One `ready` release record; no release decision.                                                     |\\n| `DR-RLS-DECIDE`            | Human authorized to make the release decision.                                                                                     | Ready release record, assessed contract obligations, and the exact release or reject decision.                    | Decision on that release record only.                                                                |\\n| `DR-EXTERNAL-ACTION`       | Human authorized for the exact external action. A Human or Agent may execute the authorized operation.                             | Action, immutable target, destination, prerequisites, and recovery conditions.                                    | Authorization limited to that operation; the subsequent tool result records its actual effects.      |\\n| `DR-RELATED-RECORD-SELECT` | Human or Agent; no decision authority is needed for inspection.                                                                    | Exact existing artifact ID.                                                                                       | Read-only selection.                                                                                 |\\n| `DR-REMEDIATION-SCOPE`     | Human authorized to change the affected definition or work scope.                                                                  | Failed criterion and proposed bounded correction.                                                                 | Explicit authority for the revised scope.                                                            |\\n| `DR-DECISION-DISPOSE`      | Human accountable for the artifact blocked by the decision; for a deviation, the human accountable for the affected specification. | Open or deferred decision, selected option or disposition, exact reason, and any required revisit trigger.        | Recorded disposition and any declared paired-risk effect. Other blocked artifacts keep their states. |\\n\\nFor a lifecycle decision, the `--decision` value identifies its actual\\ndecision-maker. When an agent applies a human decision, that value is the\\nhuman who made it. For start and completion under approved execution, it is\\nthe actual Human or Agent executor. Supplying either identity does not grant\\nauthority. Preparation `--owner` values identify the preparation actor, not\\na future assurance or release decision-maker.\\n\\nFor `decide`, when an owner label differs from the actual human's identity,\\nuse `--authority-owner OWNER` only after establishing that human's authority\\nfor the exact decision. Keep `--decision ACTOR` as the actual human. The\\nevaluator checks that OWNER holds the existing decision right and records it\\nseparately as `authority_owner`. With the option absent, ACTOR must itself\\nmatch an eligible owner. The command does not authenticate either claim or\\ngrant consent. Do not infer a binding from the decision's own owners, an\\nearlier approval, Git configuration or the agent's identity.\\n\\nSilence, elapsed time, a commit, a pull request, tool execution, or a passing\\ncheck MUST NOT count as a human decision. A rejection MUST include a non-empty\\nreason. Supersession MUST name exactly one eligible successor. The decision\\nand its resulting state MUST remain specific to the selected artifact.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"6ecaeb29429bcaa4eac99857f2f921e270c4865d1edeec96f536d09eb6c3130a\"}, {\"heading\": \"delegation-and-separation\", \"title\": \"Delegation and separation\", \"anchor\": \"delegation-and-separation\", \"start_line\": 111, \"end_line\": 133, \"content\": \"## Delegation and separation\\n\\nA human decision right may be delegated only to another human who is permitted\\nto hold it. The delegation MUST name the right, its scope, the granting human,\\nand the receiving human. It MUST be recorded before the delegated decision.\\nThe receiving human MUST decide under their own identity.\\n\\nAn instruction for an agent to apply a recorded human decision delegates the\\nexecution of that command. It does not delegate the decision itself. The\\nagent MUST NOT supply a new answer, broaden the targets, or claim the human\\nmade a decision that was not given.\\n\\nRepository policy MAY require different people for conflicting decisions on\\nthe same candidate. When it does, the same human MUST NOT hold both decisions\\nfor that candidate. A human MUST NOT approve their own architecture decision\\nassessment unless they explicitly hold approval authority for that artifact\\nand the separation rules permit it. Drafting an assessment grants no such\\nright.\\n\\nWhen the accountable human or required authority is absent or ambiguous, stop\\nthe affected decision. State the exact right and artifact needing an owner.\\nDo not substitute an available human or agent merely to fill the field.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"f07389fbbe529ad544d8b7d7f18894bfa45f0ec69996c93ed8372416b55ddf91\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/RESULTS.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-authority38-20261012\\\\claude-341\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\RESULTS.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"090e0172a78ebedfe4cdbe98e74c171e50215766a4ebbd671844a89af3f72e6e\", \"bytes\": 5242, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"read-this-when\", \"title\": \"Read this when\", \"anchor\": \"read-this-when\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 6, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Read this when\\n\\nReport a governed result, handle a refusal or recover an uncertain operation.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"680ff724251b96e21b52d9104a0e6f2806e6f4269237b555518e7d72d20463a7\"}, {\"heading\": \"before-this-action\", \"title\": \"Before this action\", \"anchor\": \"before-this-action\", \"start_line\": 7, \"end_line\": 10, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Before this action\\n\\nRead only the actual selected result and evidence needed to establish its effects. Before eligible English reporting, read [COMMUNICATION.md#communication](COMMUNICATION.md#communication) if it is no longer in context.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"22e8f880f3e2c5192c3f6abf5f63c7542b51647503ba0fb877c5acb70c7a5837\"}, {\"heading\": \"report-a-lifecycle-result\", \"title\": \"Report a lifecycle result\", \"anchor\": \"report-a-lifecycle-result\", \"start_line\": 13, \"end_line\": 46, \"content\": \"### Report a lifecycle result\\n\\nAfter a lifecycle stage completes or reaches a stop condition, obtain the\\nselected evaluator's schema-2 result. The result controls the reported scope,\\neffects, blockers, final state, required decision, alternatives, and next step.\\nThe human or agent MUST NOT recompute those fields.\\n\\nFor a human-readable report, lead with the outcome or required decision. The\\nwording and headings may vary, and empty fields may be omitted. The report\\nMUST preserve:\\n\\n1. The actual artifact IDs and whether the selected operation completed or\\n   was blocked.\\n2. The observed effects, separately from expected effects that did not occur.\\n3. Every exact blocker and every material non-effect. In particular, do not\\n   imply approval, a transition, verification, release, a Git action,\\n   publication, deployment, or operation that did not occur.\\n4. The final lifecycle state and the accountable human or agent. When a\\n   decision is required, name the exact decision and authority needed.\\n5. Exactly one current typed procedure step from the evaluator.\\n6. The command's argument values and boundaries, or the operative meaning of\\n   the suggested response.\\n\\nOnly complete alternatives declared by the workflow may be shown. Present them\\nseparately from the primary next step. Do not invent an effect, authority,\\nblocker, decision, or alternative. Do not add unrelated repository findings\\nto the selected result. Do not replace the returned next step with an\\nopen-ended question about what to do next.\\n\\nAutomation MUST read the structured JSON result. New results declare\\n`digest_format = \\\"machine-fields-v1\\\"`. This digest binds machine fields such\\nas candidate identity, scope, state, gates, and command arguments. Explanatory\\nwording is excluded. Older retained results keep their original digest format.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"d12f5fc7e6190762622666754fde9b70e658249ceb41f2fdbf48e5e0edbd0f8f\"}]}]}",

  "content": "{\"resources\": [{\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/EXECUTE_WORK.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-authority38-20261012\\\\claude-341\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\EXECUTE_WORK.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"f094e7bc796ba1002c3fb2848adaccce6c4887e3d92503911bcafa4504a644fe\", \"bytes\": 11979, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"read-this-when\", \"title\": \"Read this when\", \"anchor\": \"read-this-when\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 6, \"content\": \"## Read this when\\n\\nThe evaluator selects work start, implementation or completion under matching authority.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"b7fc3711b437c73d3cc5cce46c0a8bb7f1bc3c8c98ba650b4b046950d69a7652\"}, {\"heading\": \"before-this-action\", \"title\": \"Before this action\", \"anchor\": \"before-this-action\", \"start_line\": 7, \"end_line\": 10, \"content\": \"## Before this action\\n\\nRead the selected work order and governing artifacts returned by the evaluator. Before using its execution grant, read [AUTHORITY.md#authority-from-work-approval](AUTHORITY.md#authority-from-work-approval). For a scope finding, read [RESULTS.md#if-a-procedure-is-blocked](RESULTS.md#if-a-procedure-is-blocked).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"5581ca4e192b2a3847952cdb19582916ed96242a14941b1e5a8a610be996e1dd\"}, {\"heading\": \"start-the-selected-work\", \"title\": \"Start the selected work\", \"anchor\": \"start-the-selected-work\", \"start_line\": 70, \"end_line\": 104, \"content\": \"### Start the selected work\\n\\n**Inputs:** The approved work order (WO), actual executor identity, and\\ncurrent execution context.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** The selected work order (WO) becomes `in_progress`\\n  with a lifecycle-history entry when start succeeds.\\n- **Transient working material:** Start-check and transition results.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. If the work order (WO) is already `in_progress`, inspect the recorded start\\n   and resume unapplied work. Do not replay the start transition.\\n2. Otherwise, run start preflight under the existing approval.\\n3. Preview the transition to `in_progress` using the actual executor identity.\\n4. Apply the same transition only when preflight and the preview pass.\\n5. Inspect the resulting state.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase start --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=in_progress --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=in_progress --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\n**Completion:** The selected work order (WO) is `in_progress`, or its start\\nis blocked with an explicit reason. Existing approval covers a permitted\\nstart; no duplicate human start decision is required.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Implement the approved scope](EXECUTE_WORK.md#implement-the-approved-scope) performs the authorized implementation.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"2275126fa8b418eccbaacb1972d0140709337470ba8d190b6566fd16854328d1\"}, {\"heading\": \"retain-the-implementation-evidence\", \"title\": \"Retain the implementation evidence\", \"anchor\": \"retain-the-implementation-evidence\", \"start_line\": 155, \"end_line\": 210, \"content\": \"### Retain the implementation evidence\\n\\n**Inputs:** The implemented changes, verification contracts (VER), required\\nrepository checks, and work-order evidence locations.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Retained evidence files:** Actual check results, review findings, their\\n  resolution, and Git-derived handoff evidence at authorized evidence paths.\\n- **Formal artifacts:** Evidence references may be added where the work order\\n  (WO) requires them. No lifecycle state changes here.\\n- **Transient working material:** A summary of remaining evidence gaps.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Run every required check using its specified inputs and environment.\\n2. Retain the command arguments, working directory, runtime identity, exit\\n   status, output, and relevant file paths or digests.\\n3. Preserve failed results alongside later corrections and successful reruns.\\n4. Retain the material diff-review findings and their resolution.\\n5. Confirm that all required evidence files exist at the locations named by\\n   the work order (WO) or verification contract (VER).\\n6. Run review preflight.\\n7. Run the Git-derived handoff check with the trusted `BASE` from [Establish the execution context](EXECUTE_WORK.md#establish-the-execution-context).\\n8. Inspect its written evidence and reported findings. Correct in-scope gaps\\n   and rerun affected checks.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase review --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint handoff --from-git BASE --json\\n```\\n\\nFor ordinary files listed in the work order\\u2019s `evidence_paths`, confirm that\\neach file exists inside the repository and contains the required evidence.\\nThese files do not need a machine header. Use the supported evidence command\\nfor generated packets; do not edit their evaluator-owned binding fields.\\n\\nThe Git-derived handoff command writes retained evidence. Confirm its output\\npaths are covered by the approved preparation scope. If the evaluator calls\\nfor creating or rebinding the handoff evidence packet, use:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl evidence REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint handoff --json\\n```\\n\\nThis command also writes a file. It does not run tests or make an unsupported\\nclaim true. Rerun the required handoff check after correcting the evidence gap.\\n\\n**Completion:** Required implementation checks pass and the actual evidence\\nis retained, or the exact missing or failed checks remain visible.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Record implementation completion](EXECUTE_WORK.md#record-implementation-completion) requires passing handoff results. Verification later\\nassesses these retained files; they are not disposable planning notes.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"4cccfe03ac5fe219da997051bb91053576f18e67e834f0036f93e9856e8f12a2\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/AUTHORITY.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-authority38-20261012\\\\claude-341\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\AUTHORITY.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"da7202bf8d1beee631e9b4b5ba01c04b440fbf76d200ccbccbb8781a59f88431\", \"bytes\": 16163, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"authority-from-work-approval\", \"title\": \"Authority from work approval\", \"anchor\": \"authority-from-work-approval\", \"start_line\": 134, \"end_line\": 163, \"content\": \"## Authority from work approval\\n\\nWork-order approval authorizes execution of its approved scope: start,\\nimplementation, local edits and commits, required checks, evidence capture,\\ncompletion recording, and required verification-record preparation. A Human\\nand an Agent MUST use the same local approval, scope, and evidence checks.\\nContinue these operations without asking for the same permission again while\\ntheir scope and conditions still match. Record execution under the actual\\nexecutor's identity.\\n\\nApproval leaves the work order `approved` until its start transition is\\napplied. Selecting and scheduling work remain explicit. New approvals need\\nno separate execution delegation table or second start approval. A preliminary\\nmerge and live CI are not local execution requirements; CI still applies at\\nintegration and publication.\\n\\nThe grant does not include changed scope, acceptance of the result, release,\\nmerge, publication, or other external action. Reuse an existing explicit\\nauthorization for such an action only when its exact target and conditions\\nstill match.\\n\\nHistorical approvals keep their original meaning. Earlier explicit execution\\ngrants remain usable under the same scope checks. An older approval without\\nsuch a grant needs an authorized amendment for the remaining execution.\\nCurrent metadata or an actor label MUST NOT manufacture that historical grant.\\nIf the installed release cannot record the required authorization, stop the\\nremaining execution and report the missing historical grant and the lack of\\na supported amendment operation. Do not insert invented grant fields or\\nrewrite the old approval.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"a0ce00fccb8c1c8833455c14fe3ec75eaf01c597f0a1f46a24f7962ab1958f8c\"}]}]}",
  "is_error": false
}

## Event 569: assistant text

The diagnostic ran end to end: both required refusals happened and left nothing changed, and the valid handoff for the new work order was accepted. Everything is test data in the private sandbox. Git stays authoritative, and nothing here verifies real work or authorizes a release.

**Setup.** The service status matches the expected combination: evaluator 0.22.1, client 0.22.2, qualification candidate `ee86a842…`, `test_copy: true`. I imported the source fixture (commit `5820415…`) and opened a draft context. That context had to be anchored on the imported WO-P3-900, because naming a new work order was refused with 404 `HAG_REMOTE_UNKNOWN_IDENTITY`. No decision was applied to any `-900` record.

**New records.** I wrote INT, CAP, REQ, SPEC, VER and WO-P3-001 under `docs/engineering/lifecycle-pilot/`. All passed the evaluator's draft checks with nothing marked incomplete; the evaluator does not assess content. My own review against the type checklists found them complete. The work order's scope is the six definition files plus one evidence file. `src/greeting.py` is excluded because no source change is permitted.

**What happened, in order:**

1. **Cypher read** (context version 12, complete): confirmed WO-P3-001 links to the new requirement, specification and verification contract.
2. **Stale preview:** I previewed the approval, then revised the verification contract, then applied the old preview.
   - **Refused:** 409 `HAG_REMOTE_STALE_PROJECT`. The project version guard failed: expected 14, actual 15.
   - A second Cypher read showed all six records still `draft` and the project still at version 15, so nothing changed.
3. **Approval:** a fresh preview passed all gates and the apply was accepted. All six records are `approved`, with `test-owner` as the decision label. I kept this step's Git head `234100a1…` as the handoff base.
4. **Start:** preflight was ready, gates passed, and WO-P3-001 moved to `in_progress` under `test-executor`.
5. **Greeting check:** `assert-greeting` exited 0 with `exact greeting passed`. It ran on the local test host against the fixture file.
6. **Out-of-scope handoff** (evidence placed at `docs/notes/…`):
   - **Refused:** 422. The evaluator blocked it with `QGP-G4I-PATHS: WEX201: changed path is outside execution scope`, and nothing was written.
   - A Cypher read confirmed nothing changed.
7. **Valid handoff:** previewed, all 9 changed paths were in scope, and the apply was accepted. The project is now at version 18 and WO-P3-001 is still `in_progress`.

The evaluator's next step would be to mark the work order `implemented`. I didn't do that because the task forbids completing the work. I also did not create verification or release records, a release contract, risks, or any export or publication.

**Errors and limits:**
- I hit 5 tool-call errors and recovered from each: a missing help file, three unsupported instruction selectors and one wrongly quoted read pointer. The 3 service refusals are counted separately.
- One staged reference, `harnessctl-reference.md#concise-hosted-authoring`, was not available and I did not read it.
- The stale apply reported only the version-guard failure. I didn't observe what the service says for an outdated preview digest submitted with current versions.
- The greeting check ran against the fixture file, not a verification candidate. Its record has no Python version and makes no type check. The commit-bound verification the work order requires is still outstanding.

All requests and full results are retained in `work/` as the numbered `r*` records. The read trace is in `work/r34-observations`; that snapshot was taken before the report was written.

Files are in `work/`:
- native-report.md
- native-observations.json
